# Compiler Workspace Borrow Source Checkpoint

The build/cache goal and ticket remain active. This checkpoint qualifies the lower retained-operation implementation, copied-owner refusals and inspected compiler factory Source routing. It does not qualify successful Native execution, overlap, physical capacity, compiler performance, platform setup, default SDK or publication. All of those gates remain HOLD. Independent review is pending: all three requested execution agents are terminal with the application network-permission-revoked error; no alternative network/review channel was used.

## Result And Consumed Routing

The actual compiler workspace and reentrant writer now use a private bounded retained operation lifetime. The Native lifetime constructor authenticates the actual reservation and compiler-workspace kind before binding its private owner. A borrow rechecks that exact allocation before callback effects and after accepted logical descendants, without taking the whole compiler writer or sampling free blocks. Its custody checker requires a real accepted frame and exact base owner. Existing cold writes enroll in an already active writer before any await, then retain workspace custody and actual headroom/writer exclusion. Actual lease close preserves the first failure, including undefined.

The actual compiler Store consumes the lifetime-supplied canonical phase directly. Its ArtifactCache factory authenticates the private Store/Original/reservation/refinement, captures the closed policy, consumes a real workspace borrow, checks the private accepted frame, then passes the exact canonical callback control to its privately retained partition operation. No copied or structural Native/Store owner is accepted. The upper state constructor, parent-root Set and Script-wide writer remain; no blanket concurrency claim or guard removal follows from this cut.

The domain-neutral helper admits at most 1024 cumulative operations with depth 64. Each accepted frame retains its own descendants, closes admission when its callback returns and preserves first observed subtree/global failures. The nearest retired frame cannot regain root admission. Genuine unrestricted control aliases reconcile with the current frame; stronger inherited restrictions must remain present. It issues no Native provider, image, Store, clock or Original.

## Genuine Counterexamples

- Missing lifetime module: actual RED before implementation.
- Detached intermediate: a child of an operation whose callback returned could not start its own descendant; actual causal RED retained. Per-frame pending drainage fixed it without replacing the Original or changing budgets.
- Retired frame: a late continuation using an ancestor control regained root admission; session 21443 terminal one, actual child status zero and two effects instead of the expected one/refusal. Preserving the nearest retired frame fixed it.
- Native borrow constructor/factory: Source RED2/RED3 required the actual retained constructor, private accepted-frame check and borrow-to-partition control. Copied Native/Store owners remain negative-only; no successful Native substitute was constructed.
- Cross-lifetime GREEN2 log is prospectively named but terminal one solely because the new vector expected peak two. Actual Node files and independent SQLite agreed on three nested callbacks/peak three. The authored expectation was corrected; runtime and budgets were unchanged.

## Final Verification

Prototype and release each returned terminal zero: 23 lifetime tests/256 assertions plus 4 compiler borrowing Source tests/53 assertions, totaling 27/309 across two files. Each run separately retained adapter 10/216. These use genuine received Original child processes, ordinary Node filesystem observations, independent SQLite event incidence and Ajv/TypeScript schema/AST oracles. Expected failed Originals are terminal DATA in isolated genuine children; healthy parents stay live. Every Bun parent/child disables transpiler/compile caches.

Strict prototype/release each returned terminal zero with the unchanged strict flags and library arguments. The exact ordered explicit roster is 20 roots, retaining the previous 15-root prefix and appending the lower lifetime test/implementation/child plus borrowing test/capacity implementation. Four launch rows use the existing ticket script and selector; no additional executable command script was created.

The final3 census contains 1702 static relative-file identities, 12 Root-owned files. Before/after the third final four gates: 0 observed identity drifts and 0 missing files. This is an observed static census, not a complete execution/runtime closure. Maximum Root-owned absolute path is 173 UTF-16 code units. The current final3 Source/strict terminals are8457/76286/99694/47212, all zero.

The first final four gates also returned zero, but their1705-before/1702-after census had six non-Root identity changes and three deleted policy files. A second runtime pair23709/65828 returned one in the existing adapter because a consumer still imported a removed preparation policy. Strict prototype9597, prospectively named red, returned zero. A subsequent read showed the shared preparation and launcher consumers had already been updated to the current schemas; Root edited neither consumer and restored no deleted policy. The third current epoch repeated the four gates after those actual consumer updates and observed no drift. The prior first-cut report identity9acdc03cfd110349446c46f49a1263d158dc1a8b3bbedb3ba1e6ee093f41b450 is superseded by this current report. Its original before/after identity evidence is retained rather than relabeled.

Four files from the preceding artifact-reader roster were already absent before the first cut; three more were removed during its gates. All seven absences are listed below without attribution to Root or a claim of unchanged preservation.
